- **lean-reviewer**: Use this agent to challenge whether a MapCairn issue, plan or pull request needs everything it adds, with no stake in the work. It follows the delete-first skill, is read-only, which a Claude Code hook enforces and other tools take as an instruction, and reports what could be deleted, merged or not built, each with a path and a line count, never a test, accessibility, privacy or security check. Give it an issue number, a plan path or a pull request number. ([MapCairn/mapcairn](https://github.com/MapCairn/mapcairn/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lean-reviewer.md) · ★2 · 5 copies)
- **lean-reviewer**: Read-only review of a change or branch. Use before committing or merging to find correctness bugs, blast radius (what else breaks), untested hotspots and risky edits. Returns ranked findings with path:line and a concrete failure scenario. Never edits files. ([rohithkh4b3kr3/multi-subagents](https://github.com/rohithkh4b3kr3/multi-subagents/blob/HEAD/agents/lean-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **lean-reviewer**: Use through /lean-review. Read-only quality review of a change or audit of a folder - correctness, load, missing tests and what to delete. - **lean-reviewer**: Only dispatch after the user explicitly invokes a cc-nexs command or skill; never auto-trigger for ordinary natural-language requests. Lean mode Reviewer. - **lean4-theorem-writer**: Use to draft or revise a Lean 4 theorem/proof (.lean) that satisfies a spec's formal-proof obligation — standalone ("prove T0xx's totals-are-nonnegative theorem in Lean") or alongside implementor's red-green loop when a story's acceptance criteria include a formal proof obligation. Writes the theorem statement and proof; never runs the compiler itself and never claims a proof verified — hand off to lean4-verifier for that. ([saptarshibasu/formal-methods](https://github.com/saptarshibasu/formal-methods/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/lean4-theorem-writer.md) · 3 copies)
- **lean4-verifier**: Use to run Lean 4 as a feedback sensor on .lean proof/spec files — type-checks and compiles them (via `lake build`, or `lake env lean` for a single file) and reports pass/fail with the compiler's own diagnostics, unresolved goals, and any `sorry`/`admit` used to fake a proof through. Read-only: never edits a .lean file or weakens a theorem to force success. Use after a story touches Lean proof files, standalone ("check the Lean proof for T0xx"), or alongside test-writer/implementor's red-green loop when a story's acceptance criteria include a formal proof obligation. ([saptarshibasu/formal-methods](https://github.com/saptarshibasu/formal-methods/blob/HEAD/.agents/agents/lean4-verifier.md) · 3 copies)

- **learn-tutor**: Tutor. Live back-and-forth chat about one sub-topic node, grounded in its wiki content and prereqs. Use on /learn chat &lt;node-id&gt;. Persists transcript + understanding signal. ([VasiHemanth/learnloop](https://github.com/VasiHemanth/learnloop/blob/HEAD/agents/tutor.md))
- **learn-verifier**: sr-obsidian:learn 하네스의 출처 대조 담당. 산출물 경로와 고정 출처 목록(sources.md)만 받아 근거 칸 하나하나를 고정 태그 소스, 실행 관측, 명세 절과 다시 대조한다. 조사 맥락은 받지 않는다. 불일치는 learn-writer에게 돌려보내고, 통과나 미해결은 리드에게 보고한다. 산출물을 직접 고치지 않는다. - **learn-writer**: sr-obsidian:learn 하네스의 작성 담당. 조사 노트(research.md)와 고정 출처(sources.md)로 시나리오 초안을 쓰고, Phase 2에서 vault의 시나리오 노트, 흐름 문서, 페어드 MD를 쓴다. learn-verifier가 돌려보낸 불일치를 고치고 재확인을 요청한다. 출처 대조 판정은 하지 않는다. - **learn-x-coach**: Question-first learning coach for any subject. Diagnoses the learner's real starting point first, introduces at most one new concept per turn, forces a guess before every reveal, and ends every session with a tangible artifact written by the learner. - **learn.claude-delta**: &gt; ⚠️ **This is an output style, not an agent.** In OpenCode, `learn` had `edit: deny` — &gt; it was *unable* to write. - **learned**: Entries moved verbatim from the top-level CLAUDE.md. Hook-owned entries live in hooks/CLAUDE.md and test-owned ones in tests/CLAUDE.md. ([noah-goodrich/borg-collective](https://github.com/noah-goodrich/borg-collective/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/learned.md) · ★1)
- **learned-distiller**: Use it at session close (or when lessons are about to be written to memory) to turn the RAW lessons that emerged into well-written, well-classified memory entries. It generalizes (incident → principle), applies the PRINCIPLE-vs-FACT criterion (R.14), routes to LOCAL / GLOBAL(domain) / TRASH, and detects duplicates proposing a merge.
